ELX-LBL-000037ELX-SNAP-001CONSTITUTIONAL_TEXTf34507cfcc034c178a424161e1bc9f704d06de5c8e575b758258751e5fb1ad9dP:42The Constitution acted like a colossal merger, uniting a group of states with different interests, laws, and cultures. Under America’s first national government, the Articles of Confederation, the states acted together only for specific purposes. The Constitution united its citizens as members of a whole, vesting the power of the union in the people. Without it, the American Experiment might have ended as quickly as it had begun.VOTER_ELECTOR_QUALIFICATION_SIGNALPROPOSED_NOT_ADMITTED

In this process, the States (which includes the District of Columbia just for this process) elect the President and Vice President.VOTER_ELECTOR_QUALIFICATION_SIGNALPROPOSED_NOT_ADMITTED
ELX-LBL-000667ELX-SNAP-008CONGRESSIONAL_AND_ELECTORALa209ff669451b5e658438ced8c6676aa38c39fca37666f87e4df2a0842eaccf9P:13The Office of the Federal Register (OFR) is a part of the National Archives and Records Administration (NARA) and, on behalf of the Archivist of the United States, coordinates certain functions of the Electoral College between the States and Congress. Acting as an intermediary, it reviews the Certificates of Ascertainment and Vote before Congress accepts them as evidence of official State action in preparation for the counting of electoral votes in Congress. In addition to posting them on this website, OFR makes the physical Certificates available for public inspection for one year following the election. After that year, the Certificates become part of the National Archives collection.VOTER_ELECTOR_QUALIFICATION_SIGNALPROPOSED_NOT_ADMITTED
